# Decoupling spiral curvature from checkpoint tolerance

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical candidate, max4 round57. This is the
single mechanism authorized by TASK.md and master_guidance06950132d6f04c7a.
No mathematical program, numerical experiment, parameter scan, solver,
formal submission, or sub-agent was used. All new assertions and all
needed baseline lemmas are proved jointly below. None is an accepted fact.

The frozen baseline is cdd7f057606aeb18, SHA256
366cd6a12c8e4f56cf3c875c104f3e9bfdc185bf26c955e4f7e83d5bef7b607c.
It is not a premise. The present object is a new complete range-extension
draft, not an edit of that baseline or a report of its independent audit.

## 1. Result, domain, and conventions

The ordering kappa<=epsilon is not needed for this support arc and its
global assembly. It was used to obtain two sufficient coordinate claims:
q(theta)<=1 and u_j>=p_j. Both can be replaced. The vertical proximal
buffer is unchanged because y_j>=q_j. The negative horizontal normal is
paid by the checkpoint threshold, explicitly as follows.

Take independently

    0<epsilon<=1/2,  kappa>0.

Define the following word-independent constants. The fresh symbol
theta_sp avoids the unrelated accepted benchmark symbol Theta.

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp,  0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                    kappa>=1,
    H_sp=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon,
    a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp.                                      (1)

The formula for T_sp agrees continuously at kappa=1. Define also

    qmax_sp=1,                                      kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
             exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]),  kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max{0,kappa qmax_sp-epsilon},
    B_sp=1+max{epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon}.                (2)

The number qmax_sp is the EXACT vertical maximum of the unchanged arc;
N_sp is a uniform upper bound on the horizontal projection deficit,
not a claimed sharp maximum of that deficit. The main construction is
valid for all parameters in the displayed domain with selected steps
b_i>=B_sp. In particular,

    B_sp=1+epsilon^(-2) whenever 0<kappa<=1.              (3)

More generally the same threshold is certified whenever
epsilon N_sp<=1. Thus the entire rectangular range
0<epsilon<=1/2, 0<kappa<=1 works, including epsilon<kappa, with exactly
the old formulas for c_sp,H_sp,A_sp when kappa<1. At kappa=1 use (1).
The general kappa>1 extension pays the displayed threshold if needed.
No assertion is made that (2) is a necessary threshold or a maximal
unchanged-threshold range.

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be ANY finite nonnegative word of its ORIGINAL
physical length n. Write it in its original order as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where s_i is the sum of the possibly empty ordinary block_i. No upper
bound is imposed on any ordinary or selected step; zeros are retained.
Let W_n(h) be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth objectives with
an attained minimum, minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star, for physical GD
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). This is W=2R.

For each i<k the backward construction in Section3, applied to the actual
ordered block_i, yields P_i,Q_i with

    0<=P_i<=c_sp s_i,  0<=Q_i<=H_sp,  P_i>=epsilon Q_i.

Put

    M_i=P_i+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon},
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                        (4)

For k>=1 and b_i>=B_sp the finite max-affine function J_sp constructed
in Section5 defines ONE objective f=env_1 J_sp on R^(k+1), fixed before
the run, starting at x_0=e_0, with

    min f=0,
    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k),
    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
       =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].           (5)

All directed smooth-convex interpolation rows, including optimum and
terminal rows, hold for its actual data. Its exact closest-distance
payoff is

    (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
       times product_(i=1)^(k-1) [(b_i-1)/M_i]^2.        (6)

This is bounded below by the fully explicit mass-only expression

    1/(1+2s_k) times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
       [(b_i-1)/(c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp})]^2.      (7)

The construction's exact payoff (6) and the lower estimate (7) are
different objects. For k=0 the usual one-dimensional hinge envelope
has exact closest-distance payoff 1/(1+2s_0).

A second realization of this SAME arc gives the source-format statement
for the entire parameter domain kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, with threshold

    b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)),

and no N_sp threshold. Its specified-minimizer payoff is EXACTLY

    1/[2(1+s_k)] times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
       [b_i/(c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.             (8)

Here the specified minimizer is 0 at initial distance1. Section7 also
gives an exact finite algebraic formula for that realization's TRUE
closest-minimizer radius, so the possible distinction is not hidden.
Its true-distance payoff is (8) divided by that squared radius, which
is positive and at most1. The main realization already has the simple
closed radius (5). No radius inflation is used in either bound.

For every L>0 replace the displayed objective by L times that objective.
The step h_t/L then produces the same physical trajectory and payoff.
Neither dimension growth nor smoothness normalization introduces an
unreported factor.

## 2. The unchanged support arc: exact extrema and normals

For 0<=theta<=theta_sp set

    e_sp(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t_sp(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),
    v_sp(theta)=R_sp(theta)e_sp(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n_sp(theta)=e_sp(theta)+kappa t_sp(theta).

Let K_sp be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. Its endpoints
are (c_sp,0) and (epsilon,-1). Direct differentiation gives

    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta).

Thus epsilon<=p<=c_sp and q>=0 for every kappa>0. Also
q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon. The maximum of q is at the endpoint if
kappa<=epsilon, and otherwise at theta_c=arctan(1/kappa). This proves
the exact formula (2). In the latter case qmax_sp>1. More explicitly,

    log qmax_sp = integral_(epsilon)^kappa
                         (kappa-t)/(1+t^2) dt,
    (kappa-epsilon)^2/[2(1+kappa^2)]
       <=log qmax_sp<=(kappa-epsilon)^2/2.              (9)

These follow by differentiating the explicit logarithm in its lower
parameter and integrating. They quantify the genuine overshoot; it
is not discarded as a per-checkpoint asymptotic error.

For delta=phi-theta in [-theta_sp,theta_sp],

    <n_sp(theta),v_sp(phi)>/R_sp(theta)
       =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. The maximum
is1 at delta=0, since theta_sp<pi/2. Moreover
<n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta). Hence n_sp(theta) is an outward
supporting normal to K_sp at v_sp(theta), for EVERY positive kappa.
At v_sp(0), the vector (0,1) is also supporting, because q>=0.

The horizontal component of the actual normal is cos theta-kappa
sin theta. It decreases from1 to (epsilon-kappa)/sqrt(1+epsilon^2).
It is negative exactly when kappa>epsilon and theta>theta_c. The
vertical component is -sin theta-kappa cos theta<0 throughout the arc.
These signs, not an assumed coordinate monotonicity, are used below.

## 3. Arbitrary nonnegative ordinary words and a uniform displacement

Fix an ordered ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_m, with alpha_j>=0.
Set r_m=v_m=v_sp(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Work backward. Given
r_j=(u_j,-y_j), with projected arc point v_j=v_sp(theta_j), let
alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp],                       (10)

put v_(j-1)=v_sp(0) and r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_sp(0).
Otherwise choose the unique theta in (0,theta_j] solving

    <r_j,e_sp(theta)-kappa^(-1)t_sp(theta)>/R_sp(theta)
       =1-alpha,                                       (11)

and set v_(j-1)=v_sp(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_(j-1).
These are exact implicit definitions with proved existence and
uniqueness, not executed root searches or numerical assumptions.

We prove backward by induction that v_j=Pi_(K_sp)(r_j), that

    u_j>=epsilon,  y_j>=1,  y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,          (12)

and that before a cap, including the initial endpoint, there is t_j>=0
such that

    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e_sp(theta_j)
                                  +kappa t_j t_sp(theta_j)]. (13)

At j=m these statements hold with t_m=0. In case (10), put
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and

    r_(j-1)-v_sp(0)=mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

Both coefficients of these supporting normals are nonnegative. This
proves projection at the cap. It also gives
y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c_sp), so every earlier backward step is capped.

If (10) fails, the current point cannot already be capped. The left
side of (11) is1 at theta_j by (13), and is
(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c_sp<1-alpha at theta=0. Its derivative is

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R_sp(theta)>0.

There is exactly one root in (0,theta_j]. Since u_j,y_j are positive,
(13) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j; therefore
<r_j,t_sp(theta)>=0 at the root. Equation (11) says precisely that
r_j+(alpha-1)v_sp(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).
This proves projection and (13) at the preceding index. Finally,
adding alpha(p,-q), with p>=epsilon and 0<=q/p<=1/epsilon, preserves
(12). This also covers alpha=0 without deleting that physical entry.

Let

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P<=c_sp S, and P>=epsilon Q. We next
bound Q independently of both the length and the size of the steps.

For two consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, put
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the t_sp(theta_old) component in
the exact relation r_old=r_new+alpha v_sp(theta_old) gives

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.            (14)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2, direct
differentiation gives

    A'-(d/kappa)A
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.

Thus A<=exp(d Delta/kappa), for all kappa>0. If kappa<1 then
B<=Delta/kappa and 1+dB<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Consequently

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

Telescoping from the endpoint gives
t_j<=[exp(d(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.
If kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa, so (14) instead gives
t_j<=(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa<=T_sp. No sign reversal in a
negative-d inequality is used. At an uncapped point,

    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j
                                    +kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.

A cap leaves y unchanged. Hence 0<=Q=y_0-1<=H_sp, as asserted.

Two more bounds repair precisely the places that lost kappa<=epsilon.
At every uncapped index, (13) gives

    y_j-q_j=R_sp(theta_j)t_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0. (15)

The same inequality holds at a cap because q_j=0. If the horizontal
normal is nonnegative, u_j-p_j>=0. Otherwise theta_j>theta_c,
p_j<=p(theta_c)=kappa qmax_sp, and u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=p_j.
Therefore the uniform bound is

    u_j-p_j>=-N_sp.                                    (16)

For clarity, (16) is a bound on the ACTUAL normal displacement, even
though its proof uses the separate bounds on u_j and p_j. It neither
asserts a nonnegative horizontal normal nor absorbs an error per step.

To prove (3), when kappa<=epsilon the deficit N_sp is0. Otherwise
qmax_sp decreases in epsilon, and

    qmax_sp<= exp(kappa arctan kappa)/sqrt(1+kappa^2).

The logarithmic derivative of the right side in kappa is arctan kappa,
so for kappa<=1 it is at most exp(pi/4)/sqrt(2)<2. One elementary
verification of the last strict inequality uses pi<4 and
exp(1)<=5/2+2/9=49/18<2sqrt(2), obtained by bounding the factorial
series from its third term by a geometric series of ratio1/4.
It follows that N_sp<2<=1/epsilon. This proves (3).

## 4. Finite local planes and both required zero regions

Suppress the block index. For b>=1+epsilon^(-2), take any positive
sigma such that

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                  (17)

Define

    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q,
    V_fin={0,v_sp(0),v_0,...,v_m},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.           (18)

The finite convex hull of V_fin is contained in K_sp and includes
every v_j. The projection inequalities from Section3 therefore hold
on this finite hull. At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,
the plane with slope sigma v_j is active in phi. Indeed, for every
v in V_fin, <r_j-v_j,v-v_j><=0. Thus sigma v_j belongs to the
subdifferential of phi at z_j. The proximal minimizer of phi at x_j
is exactly z_j, and grad(env_1 phi)(x_j)=sigma v_j.

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, p>0 and 0<=q<=p/epsilon. Hence

    <v,-z_circ>
       =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
       <=-p+sigma p[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=0.

It follows that phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. At (U,Y),

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0.         (19)

So phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y. Both statements remain true of its
nonnegative envelope, whose zero set is exactly that of phi.

The local trajectory starts at (1,0), follows every actual ordinary
step, and ends that block at (U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with gradient
sigma(epsilon,-1). The step b then reaches (U,Y+sigma b). Physical
first coordinates decrease and second coordinates increase throughout;
in particular x_(j),1>=U and 0<=x_(j),2<=Y before the checkpoint.
This fact uses only p>0, q>=0, not the sign of a normal.

For the ONE-envelope global assembly we additionally need bounds at
the local proximal points. Monotonicity of backward y gives
1<=y_j<=1+Q. Together with (15),

    0<= (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j]<=sigma(1+Q).        (20)

The upper bound is EXACTLY the old buffer: the overshoot qmax_sp-1
causes no added loss, because y_j-q_j>=0. Similarly, by (16),

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]
       >=sigma[epsilon(b-1)-N_sp]>=0                  (21)

whenever b>=B_sp. This is the sole additional threshold used by that
assembly. The source-format sum assembly uses physical points only
and consequently needs (19), but not (21).

## 5. One static global envelope and the physical trajectory

Use (4) and the finite planes (18) with sigma_i=1/M_i. Conditions
(17) hold. In coordinates 0,...,k set

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),
    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},
    J_sp(z)=max_(0<=i<=k) Phi_i(z),
    f(x)=min_z [J_sp(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                  (22)

This is a specified finite maximum of affine planes, including 0.
It is nonnegative and J_sp(0)=0. Its proximal minimizer is unique,
because the objective in z is coercive and strictly convex.

Here are the needed envelope properties with their proofs. Write
z=prox(x) and g=x-z; the minimizing condition is g in partial J_sp(z).
For another input x', the two subgradient inequalities give
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, so the gradient candidate is 1-Lipschitz.
For an increment w, using z as a trial point gives
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Using the actual proximal point z'
and the subgradient inequality at z gives

    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These estimates prove differentiability with gradient g, and the
global lower supporting inequality proves convexity. The function is
everywhere finite, has minimum0, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient.

At the start of block i the claimed physical state is

    x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i,
    x_i=ell_i+D_i,
    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (23)

Start from e_0 and use the local trajectory of Section4 in coordinates
i,i+1. At a candidate GLOBAL proximal point of that local trajectory,
the current Phi_i has its stated subgradient. The preceding component
has first input U_(i-1), and second input at least
ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), by (21), so is0. Earlier pieces remain in
their post-checkpoint zero regions. The next component has first
input at most0 by (20) and ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i), and second
input0. All later components, including the terminal hinge, also lie
in their pre-activation zero regions. Thus Phi_i attains the maximum
at the candidate proximal point, even in a zero-value tie. Its stated
subgradient is a subgradient of J_sp, proving that the local gradient
is the actual global gradient.

After the checkpoint, coordinate i freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i, while
coordinate i+1 is

    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves (23) by induction through the entire supplied physical
word. No component or constraint is discarded at a transition.

During the terminal block, let u be the sum of its already executed
steps; 0<=u<=s_k. Its moving coordinate is
ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. The proximal coordinate is at least ell_k,
since D_k-(u+1)delta_tail>=0. All old pieces vanish there. The actual
gradient is delta_tail e_k at every terminal-block query and at the
unqueried terminal point. Therefore

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].                          (24)

For ALL ordered pairs of samples a,b, include the terminal and any
minimizer, and write g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a, f_a=f(x_a).
The construction gives g_a in partial J_sp(z_a) and
f_a=J_sp(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2. Exact cancellation then yields

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
        =J_sp(z_a)-J_sp(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.          (25)

For a minimizer g_star=0 and J_sp(z_star)=0, so no extra argument or
omitted optimum row is needed. Zeros give equal actual jets and also
satisfy (25). The Gram matrix is that of the explicit real vectors
in R^(k+1), so it is positive semidefinite. There is no independent
oracle branch, reset function, or unverified interpolation premise.

## 6. Exact closest radius for the single-envelope realization

Set the first two coordinates of x_star to

    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),
    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,

and all later coordinates to0. The first component is at its center,
the next has first input0 and second input0, and every later component
is in its pre-activation zero region. The terminal hinge also vanishes.
Thus J_sp(x_star)=0. Nonnegativity and attainment in (22) show that
argmin f={J_sp=0}.

Both endpoint planes of the first component have value0 at x_star.
Moreover

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c_sp] sigma_0(c_sp,0,0,...)
         +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative by P_0>=epsilon Q_0. Every point
z of {J_sp=0} has nonpositive inner product with each active endpoint
slope after subtracting x_star. Hence
<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expanding ||e_0-z||^2 proves that x_star
is the unique closest minimizer and gives the radius in (5).
Combining with (24) proves the exact payoff (6).

For epsilon<=1 and nonnegative P,Q,

    sqrt((epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2)
       <=P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon<=M.

Also M_i<=c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}. These two estimates give
(7). For k=0, use J_sp(z)=delta max{z,0}, delta=1/(1+2s_0),
starting at1. Its nearest minimizer is0, and the same terminal
calculation gives exactly 1/(1+2s_0).

## 7. Source-format realization, with its radius accounted for

Fix the same independent parameters and now suppose only
b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)). For each i<k run Section3 on its actual
ordinary steps divided by2. In this section P_i,Q_i refer to those
HALF-step blocks, so P_i<=c_sp s_i/2 and Q_i<=H_sp. Use Section4
with local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and

    sigma'_i=1/[c_sp s_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].

Both conditions (17) hold because
P_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon<=c_sp s_i/2+A_sp.
Let phi'_i, z'_circ,i, U'_i, Y'_i be the objects in (18) for these
inputs, and put f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Define

    G_i=sigma'_i beta_i=b_i/[c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i],
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i,
    ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y'_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

The single source-format objective is

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
               f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
          +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k),              (26)

where Hub_delta(z) is0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta, and
delta z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta. This is the same spiral mechanism
assembled as a sum of local envelopes instead of a global envelope.

Each local term is convex and 1-smooth on its two coordinates. Summing
their quadratic upper inequalities with coefficient1/2, and adding
the terminal term, counts each coordinate at most twice. Thus

    F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.

To see directly that this proves a 1-Lipschitz gradient in this convex
case, apply the inequality to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and
w=-grad V(x). The minimum of V is at y, so

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.                  (27)

Adding the exchanged inequality and using Cauchy--Schwarz gives the
claimed Lipschitz bound. The function is nonnegative and F(0)=0 by
the zero regions and ell'_i>=0.

At the start of block i its frozen coordinates are
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, its current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i,
and its later coordinates are0. Along the local physical path,
x_i>=ell'_i+D'_i U'_i>=ell'_i, and
0<=x_(i+1)<=D'_i Y'_i=ell'_(i+1) before the checkpoint. Every old
local objective is therefore in its post-checkpoint zero region, and
every future one is in its pre-activation zero region. Each has zero
gradient there because it is differentiable and nonnegative. Only the
current term has a nonzero gradient. Its coefficient1/2 converts the
actual physical step h_t to the prescribed local step h_t/2. The
checkpoint leaves current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i U'_i and next
coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual GD trajectory
by induction, using no bound on a horizontal PROXIMAL coordinate.

In the terminal block the physical gradient is delta'/2. The final
coordinate above ell'_k is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution in
the terminal term gives

    F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)].                        (28)

The initial point is e_0 and 0 is an actual minimizer with distance1.
Since D'_k=product_i G_i, (28) proves the exact payoff (8) about0.
At k=0 simply use Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from initial1; (28) holds with
D'_0=1. For every ordered pair of actual samples, including optimum
and terminal points, (27) with x=x_b and y=x_a is exactly Q_ab<=0.
Thus the second realization also retains every original directed row.

Here is an exact radius formula for (26); it does not replace the
simple main radius (5). Write the two coordinates of z'_circ,i as
c_(i,1) and c_(i,2). The zero set of phi'_i is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.             (29)

To prove equality, both inequalities are necessary because the two
endpoint slopes are included. They are sufficient because every
other (p,-q) equals a nonnegative combination of (c_sp,0) and
(epsilon,-1): its coefficients are (p-epsilon q)/c_sp and q.
The envelope has the same zero set. Since (26) is a sum of nonnegative
terms, its full minimum set is the following EXPLICIT polyhedron:

    C_rad={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
               epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2), i<k;
               x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta_i=ell'_i+D'_i c_(i,1).                        (30)

It contains0. Let A_rad be the (2k+1)-by-(k+1) matrix whose first
two rows for index i are e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T, with
right sides zeta_i and epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2); the final row
is e_k^T with right side ell'_k. Let t_rad be this right-side column.
For a subset S of linearly independent rows, write

    A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
    K_S=A_S A_S^T, lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S.

Include the empty subset with value0. Then the EXACT squared radius is

    rho_source^2=dist(e_0,C_rad)^2
       =max_{S independent, lambda_S>=0} r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. (31)

This is a finite algebraic expression determined by the given blocks.
It is not an executed active-set search or a numerical optimization.

For completeness, here is a proof of (31). For any feasible x and
any lambda>=0 on S, set w=A_S^T lambda. Then
<w,e_0-x>>=lambda^T r_S, and completing a square gives
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||w||^2. At lambda=lambda_S the right
side equals r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. This proves the lower bound for every
entry of the displayed maximum. Let x_* be the projection onto the
nonempty closed polyhedron C_rad, and w_*=e_0-x_*. For every direction
d with nonpositive dot product with every active row, x_*+td is
feasible for sufficiently small t>0; thus <w_*,d><=0. It follows
that w_* belongs to the nonnegative cone of the active rows. An
elementary justification is as follows. A finitely generated cone is
closed: from any nonnegative representation one can successively
remove a linear dependence while keeping coefficients nonnegative,
until only independent generators remain; it is consequently a
finite union of closed images of orthants under injective linear
maps. If w_* were outside this cone, subtracting its projection on
the cone would give a direction d with nonpositive dot product with
every generator but <w_*,d>>0, a contradiction. Choose an independent
active subset S representing w_*=A_S^T lambda with lambda>=0.
Activity gives r_S=A_S w_*=K_S lambda, hence lambda=lambda_S and
the corresponding entry in (31) equals ||w_*||^2. This proves equality.

For k>=1 the initial point is outside C_rad: its first-coordinate
constraint is 1<=c_(0,1)=1-sigma'_0(epsilon+P_0), which fails.
For k=0, C_rad is the nonpositive half-line. Therefore
0<rho_source^2<=1 in every case, the upper bound using 0 in C_rad.
The exact TRUE-distance payoff of (26) is (8)/rho_source^2. In
particular (8) is an actual payoff about0, not a quantity secretly
divided by an enlarged or unspecified radius.

## 8. Activation costs after decoupling: an exact finite family

The new range does not remove the endpoint/minimum reset cost. We
rederive a finite witness instead of importing the old unverified
obstruction. For a positive integer M put Delta=theta_sp/M and
theta_l=theta_sp-l Delta, 0<=l<=M. With A_D=A(Delta), B_D=B(Delta)
from (14), define

    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e_sp(theta_l)
                                      +kappa t_l t_sp(theta_l)],
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D
                         +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).      (32)

Every alpha_l>=0 because exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta<=1 and all
other terms are nonnegative. Resolving along e_sp(theta_l) and
t_sp(theta_l) proves exactly

    r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v_sp(theta_l).

The supporting normal proves v_sp(theta_l)=Pi_(K_sp)(r_l). Thus the
ACTUAL finite chronological word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), started at
r_M, runs backward through these indexed states to (epsilon,-1).
There is no numerical sweep or reliance on a continuous-time path.
Set S_M=sum_l alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon and
Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Since theta_M=0,

    P_M=c_sp(1+t_M)-epsilon,
    1+Q_M=c_sp kappa t_M.                              (33)

To record exact limiting constants, define

    T_eq=[exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1]/d_sp, kappa!=1,
    T_eq=theta_sp,                                    kappa=1,
    S_eq=(1+kappa^2)(T_eq-kappa theta_sp)/d_sp, kappa!=1,
    S_eq=2theta_sp+theta_sp^2,                         kappa=1.

For every fixed positive kappa and epsilon,

    t_M -> T_eq, S_M -> S_eq.                          (34)

Here are discrete derivations of the limits. If kappa!=1 then
A_D=1+(d_sp/kappa)Delta+O(Delta^2), B_D=Delta/kappa+O(Delta^2).
For all sufficiently large M, A_D!=1, and the EXACT finite formulas
are

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-M B_D)/(A_D-1),
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.

Using M Delta=theta_sp and A_D^M->exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa) gives
(34), including negative d_sp. If kappa=1, then A_D=1+O(Delta^2),
B_D=Delta+O(Delta^2), and uniformly for l<=M the finite sum
t_l=B_D sum_(j=0)^(l-1) A_D^j equals l Delta+O(1/M).
Thus t_M->theta_sp and Delta sum_(l=0)^(M-1)t_l->theta_sp^2/2.
Substituting into the exact sum for S_M gives 2theta_sp+theta_sp^2.
The error constants here depend on the fixed parameters; the limits
are used to produce finite witnesses for each fixed parameter, not
as an error bound along an arbitrary growing checkpoint word.

For any local scale sigma, the endpoint plane (epsilon,-1) at the
specified zero-height origin imposes NECESSARILY

    sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon.               (35)

This is also an original directed-row condition, not just a convenient
zero-set proof. The endpoint sample is x_end=(1-sigma P,sigma Q),
g_end=sigma(epsilon,-1), f_end=sigma^2(1+epsilon^2)/2. For prescribed
minimum data x_star=0,g_star=0,f_star=0 its original residual is

    Q_(end,star)=sigma[-epsilon
                   +sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q)].  (36)

It is positive precisely when (35) is violated. The extra q overshoot
or a new smoothness normalization does not cancel this row.

After paying the actual horizontal linear allowance c_sp S_M, the
finite words (32) have excess

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c_sp S_M
       -> E_eq=c_sp[1+(1+kappa/epsilon)T_eq-S_eq].       (37)

Consequently any universal inverse scale c_sp S+A, with A>=0, preserving the
same endpoint and zero-height minimum requires A>=max{0,E_eq}.
With denominator c_sp S+epsilon beta+A at a fixed legal local
checkpoint beta, it requires A>=max{0,E_eq-epsilon beta}. This
accounts for the available horizontal slack BEFORE asserting a cost.
No positivity of E_eq is claimed for all large kappa.

In the useful decoupled small-curvature range
0<epsilon<=kappa<=1/2, T_eq=T_sp and (37) becomes

    E_eq=c_sp[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d_sp)T_sp
                         +kappa(1+kappa^2)theta_sp/d_sp]>0. (38)

The coefficient of T_sp is at least 1-2kappa^2/d_sp>=1/3.
Uniformly over 0<epsilon<=kappa as kappa tends to0,

    E_eq/[c_sp(kappa/epsilon)T_sp] -> 1,
    A_sp/[c_sp(1+kappa)T_sp/epsilon] -> 1,
    kappa A_sp/E_eq -> 1,
    kappa log(epsilon A_sp) -> pi/2,
    kappa log((epsilon/kappa)E_eq) -> pi/2.             (39)

For example the first ratio follows because the relative subtraction
2kappa epsilon/d_sp is at most2kappa^2/d_sp, and the remaining terms
are uniformly negligible compared with (kappa/epsilon)T_sp.
For the logarithmic limits, theta_sp->pi/2 uniformly in epsilon<=kappa,
log T_sp=d_sp theta_sp/kappa-log d_sp+o(1), and c_sp->1.
The displayed epsilon factors are essential: log(1/epsilon) need
not be negligible if epsilon is allowed to shrink arbitrarily fast.

Taking beta=1+epsilon^(-2) gives
epsilon beta=epsilon+1/epsilon=o(E_eq), uniformly in this small-curvature
range, since E_eq/(epsilon+1/epsilon) is asymptotic to
c_sp kappa T_sp/(1+epsilon^2), which tends to infinity.
Thus the source-format epsilon beta term does not erase this
activation obstruction. For every fixed parameter and a proposed
smaller A, sufficiently large FINITE M violates (35), hence (36).

This obstruction is confined to the same arc, endpoint jet and common
zero-height minimum condition. It does not refute different positive
heights, other curves, or the unrestricted W_n. The decoupled
construction itself remains viable because its scale pays A_sp.

## 9. Every loss, with an arbitrary number of selected checkpoints

For the single-envelope realization all paid quantities are visible:
the threshold B_sp, numerator b_i-1, linear coefficient c_sp, activation
constant A_sp, term epsilon b_i, and terminal factor 1/(1+2s_k).
Equations (20)--(21) prove that there is no extra factor involving
qmax_sp for each transition. Above kappa=1 the only new normal cost
in this realization is the explicit B_sp threshold. The exact radius
is (5); passing to (7) pays only the stated norm and displacement bounds.

When every s_i>0 for i<k, compare (7) to the FORMAL diagnostic

    I=product_i(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k).

This is not asserted to equal or control the benchmark. The exact
logarithm of I divided by (7) is

    Loss_env=2sum_i log[c_sp+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i]
                 -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).                (40)

It is at least2k log c_sp and at most

    2k(c_sp-1)+2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i
                                     +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1). (41)

For the source-format payoff (8), relative to the diagnostic using
the SAME terminal factor and numerator product, the exact loss is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c_sp+(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i]. (42)

It is at least2k log c_sp and at most
2k(c_sp-1)+2sum_i(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i. These finite identities
are valid for arbitrary k. They do not replace a sum over checkpoints
by a single-checkpoint error or presume a uniform little-o estimate.
If a block has sum0, the actual bounds (7) and (8) remain defined;
the corresponding diagnostic with division by s_i is not used.

The exact unavoidable coefficient contribution in both diagnostics is

    2k log c_sp = k log(1+epsilon^2)+2k kappa theta_sp.   (43)

This displays the tradeoff unlocked by decoupling. For instance, along
the ONE concrete small-parameter law epsilon=kappa^2, kappa tending0,

    c_sp=1+(pi/2)kappa+O(kappa^2),
    A_sp ~ kappa^(-2) exp(pi/(2kappa)),
    E_eq ~ kappa^(-1) exp(pi/(2kappa)),
    B_sp=1+kappa^(-4).

To check the two equivalences without discarding a constant in an
exponent, use the exact identity
c_sp exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)exp(theta_sp/kappa)
and theta_sp=pi/2-kappa^2+O(kappa^6). Formula (43) remains the
proper accumulated loss; no conclusion is drawn from its per-block
coefficient alone. These constants improve the epsilon-linked
activation exponent, but preserve an exponential in1/kappa.

Also, for ANY fixed kappa>0 and epsilon tending0, (1) has
A_sp=O_kappa(1/epsilon) while c_sp tends exp(kappa pi/2)>1.
This is a direct observation from the explicit formulas, not a
second constructed curve or a schedule experiment. Keeping curvature
fixed removes the exponential dependence on1/epsilon at the price
of a fixed coefficient loss whose product over k checkpoints is
exactly accounted for by (43). There is no uniform negligible-loss
claim over arbitrary words or checkpoint counts.

## 10. Source grounding, scope, and the remaining target

The literature grounding is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is
(Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, 2026,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2, Section3.1 and AppendixA.2--A.3.
The exact HTML was downloaded and text extracted in downloads/. Its
equation (1) uses the project's W normalization. Its equation (40)
gives F(x_n)=D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] at initial distance1; retaining the
normalizing factor2 yields 1/[2(1+s_tail)], as in (8). The paper
explicitly weakens that factor in its stated Lemma2.2. This is a source
comparison only: the spiral, its local lemmas, and both global
realizations have all been derived in this joint candidate. No external
theorem is imported as a correctness premise.

Matlas returned small-step self-contracted GD, proximal curves and
projected continuous dynamics, not the present arbitrary nonnegative
explicit GD claim. Those statements are inapplicable as a substitute
for Sections2--7 and were not used. The exact primary paper supplies
the relevant assembly context, rather than any decoupled spiral theorem.

Accepted facts04219554e41d9750 and5e1848ee0b153f5f fix the original
benchmark normalization and conditional seed interfaces. They do not
prove a single lemma of this lower construction. The original ALL-split
benchmark remains

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The unresolved ORIGINAL obligation is: for every delta>0, find a
horizon threshold independent of h such that every nonnegative word
h of every larger physical length n has a legal actual-adversary
payoff at least (1-delta)/B_n. Optimizing (6), (7), or (8) over allowed
checkpoint selections, possibly together with other accepted channels,
has not been proved to meet that obligation. The accepted phase-cover
facts and this larger parameter domain do not settle that comparison.

Outcome of this assigned round: a complete JOINT UNVERIFIED positive
extension for kappa>epsilon, in particular the full independent range
0<epsilon<=1/2, 0<kappa<=1 at unchanged threshold, and a general
kappa>0 extension with (2). The source-format sum construction works
for all positive kappa at its original doubled threshold. Activation,
transfer, smoothness, all directed rows, physical word lengths and
both radius normalizations are explicitly accounted for. No fact has
been submitted, no new result is verifier-accepted, and the original
target remains open. This consumes the one authorized analytical
round; it does not authorize self-renewal or a formal gate.

## 11. Local glossary

All symbols are defined before use above. For quick reference:
theta_sp is the endpoint angle; c_sp the initial arc radius; R_sp the
arc radial function; e_sp and t_sp its radial and clockwise tangent
unit vectors; v_sp its supporting gradient; n_sp its supporting normal;
K_sp its convex hull with0; p,q its two coordinate magnitudes;
qmax_sp its exact vertical maximum; N_sp the certified horizontal
projection deficit; B_sp the sufficient physical checkpoint threshold;
T_sp the normal-parameter upper bound; H_sp the vertical displacement
allowance; A_sp the additive activation allowance; a_sp its source
format rescaling. The normal coefficient t_j is unrelated to t_sp.
P,Q are actual ordinary-block displacements and s_i their physical
step sums. M_i,sigma_i,D_i,ell_i specify the single-envelope scales
and shifts; J_sp is its finite nonsmooth maximum and f its envelope.
Primed quantities, G_i,F and Hub_delta belong to the second realization.
C_rad,A_rad,t_rad,r_S,K_S,lambda_S,rho_source specify its exact
polyhedral closest-radius formula. T_eq,S_eq,E_eq are the limiting
constants of the explicitly defined finite equal-angle family;
they are not numerical evaluations. Q_ab denotes the original ordered
smooth-convex residual. I and the two Loss quantities are diagnostic
comparisons, not the Zhang--Jiang benchmark. C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,W_n and R_n
retain their existing project meanings.

# Centered circular components with their actual retained values

Status: ONE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED. No formal gate was
allocated or used. This document does not establish the original target.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; assigned round after55,
completed ceiling56 / attempted ceiling57. Steering: TASK.md,
master_guidance77cf019af217e744. Plan finding1001ec4e8163bf2f.
Latest guidance reread:9206de0248154e84; this assignment is unchanged.

All the new assertions and their derivations below form one draft. None
is promoted to a fact, and no unverified intermediate assertion is used
as an accepted predecessor. The only accepted mathematical input used
is fact_id04219554e41d9750, restricted to the original benchmark and its
positive finite ray limit. The accepted fact5b7ff4253c38b90a is recalled
only to delimit the already established tau=1 witness.

## 1. Result and outstanding interface

The direct convex construction below gives a single static half-sum of
shifted circular support envelopes, followed by a terminal Huber term.
It works with any finite nonnegative physical word and any chronological
checkpoint selection whose selected steps exceed2. It retains EVERY
earlier component. Their later values and gradients are evaluated on the
actual GD states; they are never reset or deleted.

The draft derives:

* the finite local construction and its exact physical half-step law;
* convexity, overlap smoothness, the entire minimum set and the exact
  closest-minimizer distance for the whole chain;
* a two-checkpoint word with changing ordinary gradients and a strictly
  positive reactivated earlier terminal component;
* two tails of equal length and equal total mass on the SAME static
  function that give different retained terminal values;
* an actual two-checkpoint construction on every sufficiently large
  H_(m,tau), including tau=1/4, with a positive earlier-component value
  immediately after the root checkpoint;
* an exact two-dimensional Huber system for its entire remaining
  physical ruler tail, with all earlier contributions included;
* a uniform, actual-function obstruction in the restricted parameter
  region beta_arc <= (1+S_m)^(-2), for every checkpoint count at least2
  and every eligible chronological selection.

The positive value just after the root checkpoint is NOT claimed to
survive the remaining H_(m-1). For fixed beta_arc, and for relevant
scalings such as beta_arc proportional to1/m, the final contribution of
the explicitly displayed Huber system remains unpaid. The small-parameter
obstruction does not cover these regimes. There is neither a complete
small-tau escape nor a complete obstruction to this new law.

In particular, the all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile minimax
question in PROBLEM.md remains OPEN. W=2R is unchanged.

## 2. Source and hypothesis-exact transfer

Source: Kaizhao Liu and Yuhan Ye, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for
Gradient Descent: The Strongly Convex Case*, arXiv:2609.26873v1 (2026),
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1 . Read Sections2.2,3.1--3.3,5 and
the actual AppendixA.1--A.3 proofs, including FactA.1.

The source's condition number is denoted K_cond here, not kappa. The
source's objective is

    ||x||^2/(2 K_cond)
       +(1-1/K_cond)/2 * sum_i Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1)).       (2.1)

Its bending lemma assumes K_cond>=4, 0<beta_arc<=1/4,
s_i/K_cond<=1/16, and positive excess factor

    chi_i=product_gap(1-h/K_cond),
    eta_i=chi_i-(1-chi_i) ell_i/D_i >0.

The lemma uses selected b_i>8. Its input dynamics, before any extra
preceding-coordinate contribution, are

    (X,Y)^+=(1-h/K_cond)(X,Y)
              -(1-1/K_cond)h/2 * grad Phi_i(X,Y).      (2.2)

For repairs and bridges, a nonnegative term (h f_j,0) is additionally
allowed. This term is an actual derivative of the preceding component,
not a freely injected control. The source's bending lemma requires it
to vanish at active queries. Its global induction obtains this from
the preceding component's OUTPUT derivative vanishing above a
threshold. It does not assume all earlier components vanish.

The source's repair uses a Huber ridge with

    delta_H=D_i eta_i/[2+(K_cond-1)(1-chi_i)].

The bridge raises the output-isolation threshold and pays an extra
delta_H in outgoing amplitude. In the directly reconstructed convex
version, these corresponding algebraic quantities would be

    delta_H=D_i/(s_i+2),
    repair threshold=s_i delta_H/2,
    repair outgoing amplitude=b_i delta_H/2,
    bridge threshold=D_i/2,
    bridge outgoing amplitude=(b_i/2-1)delta_H.         (2.3)

Equation(2.3) is explanatory bookkeeping, not an invoked lemma. This
draft uses NO repair or bridge, so none of these optional gains is
included in its payoff. All selected positions below are actual
bending checkpoints. Zero additional repair positions are consumed.

We do not obtain the present function by letting K_cond tend to
infinity in the source's error bound. Sections3--6 construct a finite
convex function and its trajectory directly, with no quadratic term.
The source's contraction factors are thereby absent for a proved
reason: the displayed new function has none. Its remaining coefficient
1/2 is kept in every physical update. The exact convex transfer needs
b_i>2, proved below; this is not an unproved weakening of the source
lemma's finite-K_cond hypothesis.

The source's relative function error divides by F(x_0)-F_*. Our payoff
divides by the TRUE closest-minimizer squared distance and multiplies
the gap by2. The radius is derived in Section6; no source error ratio
or source radius is transferred.

Prior findings717b541e05f4484f and172014083da57ddf respectively concern
two specific scalar channels and at most one checkpoint of the old
spiral realizations. The old realizationc8fcf4ced5a34046 keeps earlier
components identically zero on its prescribed trajectory. None is a
premise for an upper bound on the present retained-value functions.

An exact text recall for arXiv:2609.26873 located the earlier optional
source lead9aea0cdec5a59258 and the current steering, but no completed
identical source-transfer mechanism in the shared stores. This is a
record of the search, not a claim of mathematical novelty.

## 3. A finite circular body and its elementary envelope

Fix 0<beta_arc<=1/4. Write beta=beta_arc locally in Sections3--10,
always distinct from beta_word in the ruler definition. Set

    R_circ=1+beta,
    c_arc=beta+sqrt(1+2 beta),
    p(q)=beta+sqrt(R_circ^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).             (3.1)

The circular center is (beta,beta). K_ctr is fresh notation; the project glossary K_arc denotes an older
spiral body and is not changed. Its endpoints are (c_arc,0) and
(beta,-1). Its containing closed disk also contains0, because
2 beta^2 <= (1+beta)^2. Thus the circular radial vector
(p(q)-beta,-q-beta) supports K_ctr at v(q). The normal (0,1)
also supports at the horizontal endpoint.

Every (p,-q) in K_ctr satisfies

    0<=p<=c_arc, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta q,
    and (p,0) belongs to K_ctr.                       (3.2)

The last assertion follows because the whole horizontal segment from0
to(c_arc,0) is in K_ctr. On the arc, p>=beta and q<=1, so the third
inequality holds there and hence on the convex hull.

For a compact convex body C containing0 define the finite function

    E_C(r)=max_(g in C) [<g,r>-||g||^2/2].            (3.3)

Completing the square shows that its unique maximizer is Pi_C(r),
the Euclidean projection onto C. The projection inequalities at r,s
imply

    ||Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s)||^2
        <= <Pi_C(r)-Pi_C(s),r-s>.                    (3.4)

In particular the maximizer is1-Lipschitz. Bounding the increment of
(3.3) above and below using its maximizers at r and r+d shows that the
linear part is <Pi_C(r),d> and the remainder is bounded by ||d||^2.
Consequently E_C is differentiable with gradient Pi_C and is1-smooth.
It is convex as a maximum of affine functions, and nonnegative because
0 is allowed. The projection criterion is

    g=Pi_C(r) iff <r-g,w-g><=0 for every w in C.      (3.5)

Its zero set is the polar cone of C: if a support value is positive,
a sufficiently small positive multiple of that supporting vector gives
a positive value in(3.3); if all support values are nonpositive the
maximum is0.

The cone of K_ctr is generated by its two nonzero endpoints, since

    (p,-q)=[(p-beta q)/c_arc](c_arc,0)+q(beta,-1).

Therefore

    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0
        iff U<=0 and beta U-V<=0                    (3.6)

for every sigma>0.

We also use the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0                    if z<=0,
                 z^2/2                if 0<=z<=delta,
                 delta z-delta^2/2    if z>=delta,   (3.7)

where delta>0. Its derivative is min(delta,max(z,0)); hence it is
convex, nonnegative, differentiable and1-smooth.

## 4. Exact local trajectory for the convex physical update

Let a_1,...,a_N be any finite nonnegative ordinary block, in its actual
physical order. Define alpha_j=a_j/2 and s=sum_j a_j. These alpha_j
are local times, not replacements for physical steps. Empty blocks
are permitted.

Construct r_j=(u_j,-y_j) backwards, with

    r_N=v(1)=(beta,-1).

If alpha_j=0 set r_(j-1)=r_j and keep its arc vector. Otherwise choose
q_(j-1) in[0,1) by

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
       =max{ beta/(c_arc-beta),
               [y_j+(1-alpha_j)beta]
                    /[u_j-(1-alpha_j)beta] },       (4.1)

and put

    v_(j-1)=v(q_(j-1)),
    r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j v_(j-1).                     (4.2)

The denominator in(4.1) is positive because u_j>=beta and alpha_j>0.
The left side increases continuously from beta/(c_arc-beta) to
infinity, so this is a unique finite definition. No recurrence is
executed to prove any assertion in this document.

Here are the full checks for the backward prescription. The updates
give u_(j-1)>=u_j>=beta and y_(j-1)>=y_j>=1. The ratios in(4.1)
give q_(j-1)<=q_j: at an interior current point the current ratio is
(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta), and subtracting alpha_j beta from its numerator
and adding alpha_j beta to its denominator can only lower it. At
q_j=1 there is no upper finite ratio to check. At q_j=0, the normal
cone inequality is

    y_j <= beta(u_j-c_arc)/(c_arc-beta),              (4.3)

which makes both entries of the new maximum no greater than the
horizontal endpoint ratio. Zero steps preserve everything.

For a selected interior q_(j-1)>0, equation(4.1) and(4.2) say that
r_(j-1) and v_(j-1) are collinear with (beta,beta). Since
y_(j-1)>=1>=q_(j-1), the former lies at or beyond the latter on the
outward ray. The circular normal thus proves(3.5). If q_(j-1)=0,
cross-multiplication in(4.1) instead gives

    y_(j-1) <= beta(u_(j-1)-c_arc)/(c_arc-beta).

Then r_(j-1)-(c_arc,0) is a nonnegative combination of the circular
endpoint normal and (0,1), again proving(3.5). The final point is on
K_ctr itself. This proves for every j

    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_j)=v_j,
    u_j>=beta, 1<=y_j<=y_0, 0<=q_j<=1.              (4.4)

In particular

    u_0=beta+sum_j alpha_j p(q_(j-1)),
    y_0=1+sum_j alpha_j q_(j-1),
    u_0>=beta y_0.                                  (4.5)

The last inequality follows termwise from p>=beta q. It is strict if
there is any positive ordinary step: its predecessor q is below1, so
p-beta q>0. A positive ordinary step also gives at least one actual
change of gradient before the checkpoint, because the last positive
step has predecessor q<1 and ends at q=1 after later zero steps.

Now take incoming threshold ell>=0 and amplitude D>0, and define

    M=u_0+y_0/beta,
    sigma=D/M,
    ell_out=sigma y_0,
    A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).          (4.6)

This is one fixed finite smooth convex function. It is zero when
X<=ell and Y>=0, by(3.6), and its output derivative vanishes whenever
Y>=ell_out, for EVERY X. To prove the latter, replacing a candidate
gradient (p,-q) with (p,0) increases the objective by
q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2 when q>0. Thus the unique maximizer has q=0.

Define the ordinary states

    (X_j,Y_j)=(A+sigma u_j, ell_out-sigma y_j).       (4.7)

The initial state is(ell+D,0), and (4.2)--(4.4) prove

    (X_j,Y_j)=(X_(j-1),Y_(j-1))
                   -(a_j/2) grad Phi(X_(j-1),Y_(j-1)).

Every ordinary and checkpoint query has X_j>ell and
0<=Y_j<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. The last gradient is
sigma(beta,-1).

For ANY physical checkpoint b>2 put

    t=b/2-1,
    D_out=sigma t.                                  (4.8)

Its exact post-checkpoint state is

    X^+=A-beta D_out,
    Y^+=ell_out+D_out.                              (4.9)

Thus D_out>0, and the local function is exactly0 immediately after
its own checkpoint: its centered point is(-beta D_out,D_out), which
satisfies(3.6). A later checkpoint can lower Y and reactivate Phi.
That later effect, not a fictitious positive value at(4.9), is the
retained-value mechanism analyzed below.

## 5. One static global function and every physical update

Take any physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) and selected zero-based
indices t_1<...<t_k, k>=1, with b_i=h_(t_i)>2. Let block i contain
all actual steps strictly between t_(i-1) and t_i, with t_0=-1.
Let block k+1 be the complete suffix after t_k. No unselected step
is removed, permuted or shortened. Write s_i for its physical mass.

For i=1,...,k, apply Section4 to the actual ordered block i, always
with the same beta. Denote the resulting dimensionless data by
u_i,y_i,M_i and the local full trajectory by r_(i,j),v_(i,j).
Starting with ell_1=0,D_1=1, set

    sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i y_i,
    A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i(b_i/2-1),
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K_ctr)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)). (5.1)

Finally set delta=D_(k+1)/(1+s_(k+1)) and define on R^(k+1)

    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                        +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)).
    x_0=e_1.                                       (5.2)

All parameters in(5.2) are fixed from the predetermined word and its
selected indices before GD is run. Neither functions nor past values
are modified during the run.

Each block term is convex and1-smooth on its two coordinates. The
linear map stacking those coordinate pairs and the last scalar
coordinate has operator norm at most sqrt(2), because every coordinate
is counted at most twice. The gradient of the separable sum before
composition has Lipschitz constant1. Its coefficient1/2 therefore
makes grad F Lipschitz with constant at most1. The function is convex,
nonnegative, and F(0)=0, so the minimum is attained.

For completeness the entire physical update, including every old
component, has the following exact form. At any point x let

    (P_i(x),-Q_i(x))
        =Pi_(sigma_i K_ctr)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    P_(k+1)(x)=min(delta,max(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0)),
    Q_0(x)=0.

Then for j=1,...,k+1,

    x_j^+=x_j - (h/2)[P_j(x)-Q_(j-1)(x)].           (5.3)

This is the ordinary Euclidean gradient of(5.2). In particular, old
negative output derivatives produce the nonnegative contributions in
the adjacent input coordinate; their magnitudes are the projections
of the SAME fixed components.

The active-coordinate induction is exact even when older values are
nonzero. At the start of block i its input is ell_i+D_i and all
coordinates after it are0. The local states(4.7) have x_i>=ell_i.
For i>1 this makes Q_(i-1)=0 by the preceding component's output
isolation property. Components before i-1 involve neither active
coordinate. The next component has input at most ell_(i+1), output0,
so its value and gradient vanish. All further components also vanish.
The terminal Huber is inactive if it is next. Equation(5.3) therefore
reduces on the two active coordinates to the physical half-step law
already proved. The checkpoint produces(4.9), establishing the next
incoming state. Earlier coordinates still obey(5.3); nothing in this
induction assumes their values or gradients are zero.

During the final block, x_(k+1) remains above ell_(k+1)+delta. Indeed
its exact excess after accumulated physical mass s' is

    D_(k+1)-s' delta/2 >= delta, 0<=s'<=s_(k+1).

Consequently Q_k=0, the terminal derivative is delta, and this formula
is its actual update. Earlier coordinates continue following(5.3).
The exact terminal value is

    2F(x_n)=sum_i Phi_i(x_(n,i),x_(n,i+1))
                          +D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+s_(k+1))]. (5.4)

The first summand in(5.4) is an equality term, not discarded mass.
The second term alone is a lower bound and is never used as an upper
bound for(5.2).

For general prescribed L>0, use f=L F. Then grad f=L grad F and
x^+=x-(h/L)grad f=x-h grad F. Thus the physical word is exactly h,
the function is L-smooth, and all normalized formulas below are
unchanged.

All directed smooth-convex interpolation rows hold because this is an
actual static convex1-smooth function. One can derive the inequality
directly: for V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, its minimum is at y. The
1-smooth descent bound at x and displacement -grad V(x) gives

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
        >= ||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.

Relabeling x,y gives each ordered row, including minimizers and the
terminal point. Actual Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite.

## 6. Entire minimum set and true closest radius

From(3.6), the full minimum set of(5.2) is exactly the polyhedron

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i,
               x_(i+1)>=beta(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
               x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.                 (6.1)

It contains0. This proof uses zero sets of the actual nonnegative
summands, not an independently prescribed candidate minimizer.

For the first component,

    A_1=sigma_1 y_1/beta,
    1-A_1=sigma_1 u_1,
    ell_2=beta A_1.

The inequality u_1>=beta y_1 from(4.5) implies
0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). The point

    x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0)                       (6.2)

belongs to(6.1): the first two constraints are equalities, the next
input is precisely ell_2, and every later input is0 with a nonnegative
threshold. The terminal constraint is satisfied as well.

For any x in(6.1), if x_1<0 then ||e_1-x||^2>=1. If 0<=x_1<=A_1,
the constraint x_2>=beta x_1 yields

    ||e_1-x||^2 >= (1-x_1)^2+beta^2 x_1^2.

This quadratic decreases up to x_1=1/(1+beta^2), so its minimum on
[0,A_1] is at A_1. The point(6.2) attains that bound, which is at
most1. Therefore the exact squared closest distance is

    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2
            =sigma_1^2(u_1^2+y_1^2)>0.              (6.3)

It is independent of the later checkpoint count for THIS translated
circular law. It is not a radius formula for the old spiral chain.

The exact normalized payoff about this actual closest minimizer is

    E_arc(h,selection,beta)
       =[sum_i Phi_i(x_(n,i),x_(n,i+1))
                    +D_(k+1)^2/(2(1+s_(k+1)))]/R_true^2.
                                                        (6.4)

This is a lower witness for W_n(h), not an identity for the supremum.

## 7. Explicit two-checkpoint retained-value construction

Fix any beta in(0,1/4]. Let a physical ordinary block consist of the
single step2. In(4.1), alpha=1. Define

    T=sqrt(1+beta^2),
    q=(1+beta)/T-beta,
    p=beta+beta(1+beta)/T,
    u=beta+p,
    y=1+q,
    M=u+y/beta.                                    (7.1)

Here 0<q<1. The backward state is(u,-y), its projection is(p,-q),
and one physical step2 sends it to(beta,-1). Thus gradients change
on both ordinary blocks below.

Choose the explicit transfer amplitude

    G=1+beta+y/(beta^2 M)+(1+beta^2)/(beta M),
    b=2(1+M G),                                    (7.2)

and take the predetermined physical word(2,b,2,b), selecting only
the two b positions. The data in(5.1) are

    sigma_1=1/M,  D_2=G,  ell_2=y/M,  A_1=y/(beta M),
    sigma_2=G/M,  D_3=G^2,
    ell_3=G y/M, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta.

The five actual states, including initialization, are

    (1,0,0),
    (A_1+beta/M, ell_2-1/M,0),
    (A_1-beta G, ell_2+G,0),
    (A_1-beta G, A_2+beta G/M, ell_3-G/M),
    (A_1-beta G, A_2-beta G^2, ell_3+G^2).           (7.3)

At the first two queries the active local gradients are respectively
(p,-q)/M and(beta,-1)/M. At the next two they are G times these
vectors, in coordinates2,3; Phi_1 is zero at those queries. The
factor1/2 in the global gradient is already paid in(7.3).

For X<=A_1, the first component has the exact edge formula

    Phi_1(X,Y)=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_1)(beta X-Y)
                                      /(1+beta^2). (7.4)

To prove it, fix the negative gradient coordinate -q_phys. The body
requires p_phys>=beta q_phys. Since X-A_1<=0, decreasing p_phys to
beta q_phys increases the maximized expression. That boundary segment
is in the body for0<=q_phys<=sigma_1. Maximizing over this segment
gives(7.4); the translations cancel because beta A_1=ell_2.

At the final point in(7.3), its left margin is

    z=beta G^2-beta^2 G-G y/(beta M)
      =G[beta+(1+beta^2)/M]
      >(1+beta^2)/M.                                (7.5)

Hence Phi_1 is saturated and has strictly positive value

    Phi_1=z/M-(1+beta^2)/(2M^2)>0,
    grad Phi_1=(beta,-1)/M.                         (7.6)

The second component is0 at its own post-checkpoint point. With
empty terminal block, delta=D_3=G^2 and the exact terminal payoff is

    E_arc=[z/M-(1+beta^2)/(2M^2)+G^4/2]
                         /[(u^2+y^2)/M^2].         (7.7)

This strictly exceeds the discarded-old-value expression G^4/(2R_true^2)
for the same function and radius. It is a finite exact example; no
claim about the original minimax value follows from its large steps.

There is also a literal two-ordinary-query version of the same test,
if gradient change is required between ordinary queries themselves.
Use the block(2,2) twice. First form p,q in(7.1), put
u_mid=beta+p, y_mid=1+q and T_mid=sqrt(u_mid^2+y_mid^2), and define

    p_start=beta+(1+beta)u_mid/T_mid,
    q_start=(1+beta)y_mid/T_mid-beta,
    u=u_mid+p_start, y=y_mid+q_start, M=u+y/beta.

These are exactly two backward steps of(4.1) with alpha=1.
Indeed u_mid<1<y_mid, while beta y_mid<u_mid, so

    beta/(c_arc-beta)<y_mid/u_mid<1/beta.

Thus 0<q_start<q<1, with no endpoint clipping. Define G,b by(7.2)
using these new u,y,M, and use the physical word(2,2,b,2,2,b).
The two ordinary gradients in either gap are the distinct scaled
vectors(p_start,-q_start) and(p,-q). The checkpoint gradient is the
third vector(beta,-1). The post-checkpoint states, the minimum/radius,
and equations(7.5)--(7.7) all have exactly their displayed forms with
these substituted u,y,M. This is another explicit instance of the
SAME all-word law(5.2), with every added physical step accounted for;
it is not a changed gluing rule or an unpaid bridge.

## 8. Why a retained snapshot or gap mass is insufficient

The positive first value in(7.6) can be erased by actual subsequent
GD. More strongly, one static function has different retained terminal
values under equal-length, equal-mass tails.

For the prefix(2,b,2,b) in Section7, put

    C=(1+beta^2)sigma_1,
    z_c=C/2,
    h_enter=2(z-z_c)/[(1+beta^2)sigma_1]>0.          (8.1)

Consider the TWO full words obtained by appending respectively

    (h_enter,2,0)   and   (h_enter,1,1).              (8.2)

They have identical total tail mass h_enter+2. Thus construction(5.2)
gives exactly the SAME sigma_i,ell_i,A_i and terminal delta for the
two words: it is the same global function and the same initial point
and closest minimizer. The construction does not select any new
checkpoint in these tails.

Throughout these tails the third coordinate stays above ell_3, so the
second component has the horizontal expression

    Phi_2(X,Y)=Hub_(c_arc sigma_2)(X-A_2), Y>=ell_3.

At the prefix endpoint x_1<=A_1 and the left margin z is positive.
Also beta x_1<A_2. The first tail step uses precisely the saturated
gradient in(7.6). It reduces the margin to z_c and leaves the second
coordinate below beta x_1<A_2, so Phi_2 remains zero. Afterwards the
first component is in its quadratic region. A physical step h there
multiplies its margin by1-h/2, as follows directly from(7.4) and the
global factor1/2.

The tail(h_enter,2,0) therefore reaches margin0 and has retained
terminal value0. The tail(h_enter,1,1) ends at margin z_c/4 and has

    Phi_1=z_c^2/[32(1+beta^2)]>0, Phi_2=0.          (8.3)

The terminal Huber value is identical because its trajectory depends
only on the common tail mass. No old component, minimum, or
normalization was changed. This refutes both an unchecked claim that
the retained snapshot persists and a claim that the full payoff of
this construction is determined by the checkpoint sizes and gap
masses alone. It does not refute such a formula for the DIFFERENT
old zero-value spiral constructions.

## 9. Uniform local bounds needed for an unbounded ruler test

The following bounds are proved for the direct convex construction,
not imported as a finite-K_cond lemma. Define constants depending only
on beta by

    I_beta=sqrt(2)(1+beta)-beta,
    Y_beta=beta[exp(I_beta/beta)-1],
    U_beta=beta+(c_arc-beta)(Y_beta+beta)/beta.        (9.1)

For every finite ordinary word, y_0<=Y_beta. To check this, put
w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta). For consecutive uncapped points, the
circular normal relation is

    u_j-beta=(p_j-beta)w_j.

Taking the cross product of r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j v_(j-1) with
v_(j-1) and canceling the alpha term gives

    w_(j-1)=w_j[1+(p_(j-1)q_j-q_(j-1)p_j)
                         /{beta(p_(j-1)+q_(j-1))}]. (9.2)

If q_(j-1)=0, the needed inequality follows directly from
y_(j-1)=y_j; if both are zero it is equality. Zero steps do not
change it. The numerator equals

    integral_(q_(j-1))^(q_j) [p_(j-1)-q_(j-1)p'(t)] dt.

After division by p_(j-1)+q_(j-1), the integrand is at most
max(1,-p'(t)). Using1+x<=exp(x) and multiplying(9.2), with w_N=1,
gives

    y_0+beta <= (q_0+beta)
          exp[beta^(-1) integral_(q_0)^1 max(1,-p'(t)) dt]
       <= beta exp[beta^(-1) integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t)) dt].

The second inequality uses log(1+q_0/beta)<=q_0/beta and that the
integrand is at least1. Since
p'(t)=-(t+beta)/(p(t)-beta), its crossing of -1 occurs at
t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Integrating on the two sides gives precisely
I_beta in(9.1), proving the bound.

Whenever q_j>0, the normal relation and y_j<=Y_beta imply
u_j<=U_beta. Steps with q_(j-1)>0 form one contiguous portion in
backward order, and their sum of alpha_j p_(j-1) is at most U_beta-beta.
For these steps, p_(j-1)>=beta. Every other step has p_(j-1)=c_arc.
Consequently

    u_0=beta+c_arc s/2-E_gap,
    0<=E_gap<=c_arc(U_beta-beta)/beta.               (9.3)

Together with1<=y_0<=Y_beta, this proves the uniform, order-independent
ESTIMATE (not equality)

    M=c_arc s/2+O_beta(1).                          (9.4)

The constant is finite for each fixed beta and explicitly controlled
by(9.1)--(9.3). No uniform O(1) in beta tending to0 is asserted.

## 10. An actual two-checkpoint H_(m,tau) construction

Here all occurrences of beta without the arc subscript still mean
beta_arc. To define the physical word separately, fix tau>0 and
integer m>tau and write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    beta_word=1-tau/m,
    c_word=lambda(1+tau/m),
    a_word=beta_word/c_word,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_word c_word^j,H_j),
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1,
    S_j=sum H_j, b_j=1+a_word c_word^j.              (10.1)

Every child uses these SAME enclosing(m,tau) parameters. Only tau=1
is the previously designated original ruler family; tau=1/4 is the
requested small-tau diagnostic. Neither is the full all-word target.

Select only the first occurrences of levels m-2 and m-1. Their
zero-based physical indices are

    t_1=2^(m-2)-1, t_2=2^(m-1)-1.

The full unchanged word decomposes exactly as

    (H_(m-2),b_(m-2),H_(m-2),b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).     (10.2)

For sufficiently large m both selected steps exceed2. The two
ordinary blocks are IDENTICAL physical words, so their dimensionless
backward data u,y,M are identical. Put

    S=S_(m-2), T_1=b_(m-2)/2-1, T_2=b_(m-1)/2-1,
    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M,
    sigma_2=D_2/M, D_3=T_1 T_2/M^2,
    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2 y/M,
    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta.           (10.3)

All local states are those of Sections4--5. In particular after the
root checkpoint the actual first two coordinates are

    v=A_1-beta D_2, w=A_2-beta D_3.                  (10.4)

The first component has remained0 throughout the second gap, since
its first input was fixed below A_1 and its second was at least ell_2.
Its margin immediately after the root checkpoint is exactly

    z_m=beta D_3-beta^2 D_2-ell_3/beta.              (10.5)

Thus positive reactivation at this checkpoint is a property of the
actual physical ruler trajectory whenever z_m>0, not a new oracle
assignment of a value.

For each fixed beta in(0,1/4] and fixed tau>0 this occurs for EVERY
sufficiently large m. Here is a fully symbolic proof. Put
v_word=a_word/(c_word-2). The mass recurrence gives exactly

    1+S_j=v_word c_word^j+(1-v_word)2^j.             (10.6)

Since c_word(c_word-2)>1>beta_word, we have0<v_word<1, and
v_word tends to1. Thus, as m grows with the fixed tau,

    S_(m-2)/(lambda^(m-2)) -> exp(tau),
    b_(m-2)/S_(m-2) -> d:=lambda-2=1/lambda,
    b_(m-1)/S_(m-2) ->1.                            (10.7)

Equations(9.3)--(9.4) now give

    M/S -> c_arc/2, y/S ->0,
    D_2 -> d/c_arc, D_3 -> d/c_arc^2,
    A_1,ell_2,A_2,ell_3 ->0,
    R_true^2 ->1,
    z_m -> beta d(1-beta c_arc)/c_arc^2 >0.          (10.8)

Positivity follows, for example, from c_arc<2 and beta<=1/4.
Also sigma_1 tends to0. Hence eventually the first component is
saturated at this post-root state and has value

    Phi_1=sigma_1 z_m-(1+beta^2)sigma_1^2/2>0.      (10.9)

This is an unbounded-family retained-value phenomenon, not merely
the finite example of Section7. It is still a PRE-TAIL statement.

To make its scale explicit, retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G_ZJ(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G_ZJ(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                  (10.10)

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives the positive finite constant
Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m. No benchmark is recalculated here.
Combining that fact with(10.7)--(10.9) yields the PRE-TAIL limit

    B_(n_m) Phi_1(post-root)/R_true^2
       -> 2 Z_1 lambda exp(-tau)
                         beta(1-beta c_arc)/c_arc^3 >0. (10.11)

The denominator B_(n_m) in(10.11) is a comparison scale only. The
root checkpoint occurs before n_m, so(10.11) is NOT a final-iterate
lower bound for W_(n_m).

### The complete remaining physical tail

Throughout the actual suffix H_(m-1), the third coordinate remains
above ell_3, by Section5. The first coordinate v never increases,
because its only gradient contribution is nonnegative. Hence v<=A_1
throughout. The EXACT two old components on all these states are

    Phi_1(v,w)=Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_1)(beta v-w)/(1+beta^2),
    Phi_2(w,x_3)=Hub_(c_arc sigma_2)(w-A_2).          (10.12)

Their joint contribution to F is half their sum. For each and every
physical h in the ordered word H_(m-1), set

    q=min(sigma_1, max(beta v-w,0)/(1+beta^2)),
    r=min(c_arc sigma_2, max(w-A_2,0)).

The actual updates are exactly

    v^+=v-h beta q/2,
    w^+=w+h(q-r)/2.                                 (10.13)

The third-coordinate update is x_3^+=x_3-h delta/2 with
delta=D_3/(1+S_(m-1)). No tail step has been dropped. The flat
interval beta v<=w<=A_2 is nonempty: beta v<=ell_2<A_2. Only one
of q,r can be positive at a time, and its width A_2-beta v increases
on a left-active step and is constant on a right-active one.

Let (v_fin,w_fin) be the result of this finite, explicitly prescribed
symbolic composition; no recurrence was executed. The FULL terminal
payoff is exactly

    E_arc={ Hub_((1+beta^2)sigma_1)(beta v_fin-w_fin)/(1+beta^2)
                  +Hub_(c_arc sigma_2)(w_fin-A_2)
                  +D_3^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))] }/R_true^2. (10.14)

This proves legality and reduces the unpaid interface to an actual
two-coordinate system, but does not supply its needed terminal bound.
The same-mass counterexample in Section8 explains why replacing
(10.13) by its total mass is invalid.

For reference, ONLY the last, decoupled term in(10.14) has the limit

    B_(n_m) D_3^2/[2(1+S_(m-1)) R_true^2]
       -> Z_1 exp(-tau)/(2 lambda c_arc^4).          (10.15)

The two preceding nonnegative terms cannot be bounded above by
deleting them. Nor can the positive pre-tail limit(10.11) be added to
(10.15) without proving its survival under(10.13).

## 11. A scoped actual-function obstruction on the same small-tau words

This section controls ALL chronological selections with k>=2, but
ONLY the very small arc-shift regime stated explicitly below. It is
not an all-beta result.

Fix 0<tau<=1/4 and m>=2. Let S_m be the mass of the unchanged
H_(m,tau), and put T_mass=1+S_m. Suppose

    0<beta_arc<=T_mass^(-2).                         (11.1)

For every eligible selection with k>=2, the actual complete law(5.2)
then obeys

    sum_i Phi_i(x_(n_m,i),x_(n_m,i+1))=0,
    E_arc / [1/(1+2S_m)] <= 1/[2(1+S_m)].           (11.2)

Both the zero retained value and the upper bound concern the actual
function normalized by its closest radius. Here is the proof.

First, for any word, the sufficient inequalities

    beta^2(b_i/2-1)<=y_i for every checkpoint i      (11.3)

imply that all earlier components remain0 forever after their own
checkpoints. Indeed(4.9) then gives x_i^+>=ell_i and x_i^+<=A_i.
The next component's ordinary states have input>=ell_(i+1), and
its checkpoint also leaves that input above the same threshold by
(11.3). Thus the old component has first input<=A_i and second
input>=ell_(i+1), so(3.6) makes its value and BOTH derivatives0.
Induction leaves every old coordinate fixed. The final Huber tail
also stays above its isolation threshold, so no old component wakes.

Under(11.1), every selected b_i<=S_m and every y_i>=1, so(11.3)
holds. The exact payoff is therefore just the terminal term in(6.4).

Next M_i>=1/beta implies

    D_(i+1)/D_i <= beta b_i/2 <= r_mass:=beta S_m/2<1. (11.4)

We must pay the initial closest-radius normalization, rather than
bound D_2 and pretend the radius is1. On the arc, concavity of p(q)
gives p(q)+q>=1+beta. Therefore for the initial ordinary block of
mass s_1,

    u_1+y_1 >= (1+beta)(1+s_1/2),
    u_1^2+y_1^2 >= (1+s_1/2)^2/2.

Using(6.3) gives the exact cancellation and bound

    D_2^2/R_true^2
       =(b_1/2-1)^2/(u_1^2+y_1^2)
       <=2[(b_1-2)/(s_1+2)]^2.                    (11.5)

If the first selected checkpoint has level j, its initial ordinary
prefix contains a complete preceding H_j. Hence s_1>=S_j.
For the range in this section, 2<c_word<3. Equation(10.6) implies

    b_j=1+a_word c_word^j
        <=1+(c_word-2)(1+S_j)<=S_j+2.

Thus(11.5) is at most2. For k>=2, (11.4) and the exact terminal
formula yield

    E_arc <= r_mass^(2k-2)/(1+s_(k+1))
           <=r_mass^2.

Multiplying by1+2S_m and using beta<=T_mass^(-2), S_m<T_mass,
1+2S_m<2T_mass, gives

    (1+2S_m)E_arc
       <= beta^2 S_m^2(1+2S_m)/4
       <= 1/(2T_mass),

which proves(11.2). All ordinary steps, including unselected large
ones and zeros if present, were retained in this argument. There is
no restriction on the number or positions of the eligible checkpoints.

For fixed 0<tau<=1/4, (10.6) gives S_m/lambda^m ->exp(tau).
The accepted benchmark ray limit then shows from(11.2) that

    sup_(k>=2, eligible selections, 0<beta<=T_mass^(-2))
                   B_(n_m) E_arc ->0.              (11.6)

The supremum has exactly the scope displayed. In particular,
(11.6) does not concern fixed beta, beta proportional to1/m, one
checkpoint, other static laws, or the supremum W_(n_m).

## 12. Exact remaining interface and failed shortcuts

The main unresolved question for the explicit two-checkpoint law is
the final value of the first TWO terms of(10.14), under the precise
ordered word H_(m-1) and incoming state(10.4). To establish a genuine
small-tau escape, one must bound that full quantity, with the radius
(6.3), strongly enough to compare the final payoff with1/B_(n_m).
To close the whole new family, one must also control every eligible
checkpoint selection and every relevant beta scaling; Section11
closes only its explicitly tiny-shift portion.

Two direct attempts at shortcutting that interface fail for concrete
reasons proved in this same draft:

1. Carry the positive post-root old value unchanged to the terminal
   iterate. The first tail in(8.2) erases it exactly on the same static
   law, so a retained snapshot supplies no such lower bound.
2. Replace the ordered old-coordinate tail by its total mass and
   optimize a checkpoint product. The two tails in(8.2) have the same
   mass and length and the same global function, but distinct final
   old values. An all-history argument is necessary.

No repair of the chosen construction has been made: zero of the one
allowed repairs is used. Adding a source repair or bridge would
consume real physical checkpoint positions and alter both the
incoming states and loss factors. It is not justified as a free
restart. The present all-bending law already realizes its active
coordinates without such an operation.

The source's strongly convex exponent theorem, the older scalar
collapse717b541e05f4484f, the zero/one-checkpoint result172014083da57ddf,
and the accepted original-ruler lower witness5b7ff4253c38b90a do not
settle the displayed interface or the original uniform target.
